# Proposed OEIS comments: A301320 to A304226

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A301320

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A301320>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [6093](../papers/6001-6500/6093-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 4*a(n-1) -3*a(n-2) -4*a(n-3) +4*a(n-4) +2*a(n-6) -5*a(n-8) +2*a(n-9) +4*a(n-10) -2*a(n-11) -2*a(n-12) +a(n-14) for n>16

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 1 024 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 16. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A301796>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [8199](../papers/8001-8500/8199-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) -a(n-3) for n>7.

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 16 384 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 7. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A301921

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A301921>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [45](../papers/0001-0500/0045-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: Let k be a positive integer. The sequence obtained by reducing a(n) modulo k is eventually periodic with the period dividing phi(k) = A000010(k). For example, modulo 9 we obtain the sequence [0, 1, 3, 1, 6, 4, 0, 7, 3, 1, 6, 4, 0, 7, 3, 1, 6, 4, 0, 7, ...] with an apparent period of 6 = phi(9) beginning at n = 2. Cf. A004123. (End)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This is true. Under t = e^x - 1 the entry's e.g.f. becomes an ordinary power series with integer coefficients; that integrality kills all but finitely many terms of the Stirling expansion of a(n) modulo k, and what is left is an integer combination of the sequences n -> j^n, each eventually periodic mod k with period dividing phi(k). - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A303724>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [7043](../papers/7001-7500/7043-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) -a(n-4) -a(n-6) -a(n-7) +2*a(n-8) for n>12

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 1 024 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 12. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
